1. When directed by your commanding officer in October 1950, you will regard your-
self detached from duty at the Naval Photographic Center, Naval Air Station, Ana-
costia, Washington, D.C., and from such other duty as my have been assigned you;
will proceed to San Francisco, Calif., and report to the Commandant, Twelfth Naval
District, for transportation to Barbers Point, T.H., and upon arrival report to the
Commanding Officer, Naval Air Station, for duty.

2. You are hereby authorized to delay for a period of ten days (provided no excess
leave involved) in reporting at San Francisco, Calif., in compliance with these
erders, such delay to cout as leave. Keep the Bureau of Naval Persomel advised
Gs your address.=

From: Commanding Officer, U. S. Naval Air Station, Barber's Point
Tos CHPHOT Delbert C. NEWHOUSE, USN, 177283/8311

Subj; Change of duty
Ref; (a) BUPERS PERS Bl15J desp 261853Z of APR 1951
1. Reference (a), certified original despatch, is transcribed herewith:

"When directed by your commanding officer, about 1 May, you will
regard yourself detached from duty at the U. S. Naval Air Station,
Barber's Point, I. H. and from such other duty as may have been assigned;
will proceed to a port in which Commander, Fleet Air Activity, Japan-
Korean may be and upon arrival report for duty at the Photographic
Technical Office, Aviation Supply Branch, Yokosuka.

"Travel via government transportation is directed outside the
United States. Class TWO priority is certified for travel via govern-
ment aircraft.

"Cost of this travel is chargeable to subhead 18, MPN current on
date of detachment (1711453 for 1951 - 172153 for 1952), expenditure
account 7/110, object class 029.

2. Immediately upon reporting for duty in compliance with these orders,
or any modifications thereto, you are directed to forward to the Chief
of Naval Personnel via your new commanding officer, a copy of these
orders complete with all endorsements thereto, including the final
reporting endorsement (staff corps officers an additional copy to
cognizant Bureau).

1. Your application £of transfeF to connnissioned limited duty status
has been carefully considered by the Selection Board convened in April
1949 by the Secretary of the Navy.
I regret to inform you that your ·
name was not included among those recommended for appointment. However•
I wish to assure you that this decision is no reflection upon your per•
formance of duty in the Navy; nor will it in any way affect your present
statuso
2o
The number of applicants desiring transfer to commissioned limited
duty status has been far in excess of the number of appointments which
can be offeredo
Therefore, it was necessa._ry for the Selectio~ Board~
­
to recommend, f r om among eligible ·applicants , only those best qua.lit
~
for appointmento The decision in your case was made after a careful
review of your entire record and your qualificatio~ as compared with
1 ,
those of other applicants of your_rank and classification or enlisted
ratinge
3. The interest you have sholln in pursuing a naval career represents
an exemplary attitude of service and patriotism.
The Navy is sinc:erely
appreciative ot your contribution toward maintaining the naval eatab•
liabment at a high level of efficiency during the war, the demobiliza•
tion period• and the continuing period of world unrest.

• The acts state that death gratuity must be paid to the surviving wife (or
husband) or children, If any. I n the event of leaving no wife or children,
It may then be paid to a designated dependent beneficiary. Every member
of the service, whether married or not, should designate a beneficiary
(and an alternate, In case of prior death of the primary beneficiary), Failure
to make such designation may result In nonpayment of the gratuity.
Such designation does not affect payment to a surviving wife (or husband)
or chlldren,

From:

CW04 Delbert C. NEWHOUSE, USN, 177283/8311
Subj: Change of duty
Ref:
(a) BUPERS msg 191325Z of January 1956
1. Reference (a) is quoted below for your infonnation and compliance:
11CWO W3 DELBERT C NEWHOUSE 177283/8311 DIR DET ABT 15 FEB PROUS
ARPROREP NATECHTRAU NAS PNCLA DUTY X DELREPARUS THIRTY DAYS X
GOVTRANSDIB.VAIL Two COMLTRANS AUTH OUTUS WRERE GOVTRANS NOT AVAL X
2. In accordance with reference (a), when directed by the Commanding Officer,
Utility Squadron FIVE, about l.5 February 1956, you will regard yourself detached
from duty with Utility Squadron FIVE and from such other duty as may have been
assi~ed. You will proceed to a port in the United States and upon arrival
further proceed and report to the Commanding Officer, u. s. Naval Air Technical
Training Unit, Naval Air Station, Pensacola,Florida for duty.
3. You are hereby authorized to delay for a period of thirty (30) days, provided
no excess leave involved, in reporting in compliance with these orders, such
delay to count as leave, any portion of which may be taken prior to or after
arrival in the United States.
4. Travel via government transportation is directed outside the United States
where available. Class 'IWO priority is certified for travel via government air­
craft. Commercial transportation authorized outside of the United States where
government transportation is not available.
5. Cost of this travel is chargeable to appropriation 1761453.18 MPN 1956,
object class 029, expenditure account 741201 Bureau Control 22/31600.
6. Immediately upon reporting, copies of these orders shall be forwarded to
the Chief of Naval Personnel in accordance with Art. C-5407(4), Bureau of
Naval Personnel Manual.
7. You are hereby authorized to draw three (3) months advance pay.
8. Delivered.
B. F. ROWE
.

5 March 1954
From: Chief of Naval Personnel
To:
CHPHOT Delbert C. NEWHOUSE, USN
Naval Supply Center
Oakland, Calif.
Via:
Commanding Officer
Subj: Change of duty
1 .
When directed by your commanding officer, on or about l June 1954, you
will regard yourself detached from duty at the Naval Supply Center, Oakland,
Calif., and ·from such other duty ar may have been assigned you; will proceed
to San Francisco, Calif., and report to the Commandant, Twelfth Naval District,
for transportation to the port in which the Commanding Officer, Utility
Squadron FIVE may be, and upon arrival report to the Commanding Officer,
Utility Squadron FIVE, for duty.
2. Provided no excess leave is involved, you a.re hereby authorized to delay
for a period of ten days in reporting at San Francisco, Calif.,
in compliance with these orders, such delay to count as leave.
3. Travel via government transportation is directed outside the United States.
Class TWO
priority is certified for travel via government aircraft.
4. Cost of travel is chargeable to 1741453.18, MPN 1954, object class 029,
expenditure account 74120, Bureau Control 22/31600.
5,. Upon reporting, forward to BuPers a copy of these orders (Staff Corps Officers
an additional copy to cognizant Bureau) via new commanding officer with all endorse­
ments.
Where leave or delay is involved, keep old and new station advised of
address using Form NavPers 3036.
Copy to:

Via: Commanding Officer

Subj: Change of duty —

1. When directed by your commanding officer in October 1950, you will regard your-
self detached from duty at the Naval Photographic Center, Naval Air Station, Ana-
costia, Washington, D.C., and from such other duty as may have been assigned you;
will proceed to San Francisco, Calif., and report to the Commandant, Twelfth Naval
District, for transportation to Barbers Point, T.H., and upon arrival report to the
Commanding Officer, Naval Air Station, for duty. | | '
2. You are hereby authorized to delay for a period of ten days (provided no excess
leave involved) in reporting at San Francisco, Calif,, in compliance with these
orders, such delay to cout as leave. Keep the Bureau of Naval Personnel advised
of your address.=s= . Lee

3. Travel via government transportation is directed outside the United States.
Class TWO priority is certified for travel via government aircraft.

4, Cost of travel is chargeable to 1711453.18, MPN 1951, expenditure account 74110,
object class 029.

NOTICE TO ALL OFFICERS ON CHANGE OF STATION
In view of the extreme housing shortage in
all shore establishments it is considered
highly inadvisable to move families until
such time as the officer in question has
reported for duty and obtained suitable
quarters for his family.

INSTRUCTIONS
This form must be sworn to before an officer of the United States Navy
or Marine Corps authorized to administer oaths, or before a notary
public.
The full names and addresses of the beneficiaries must be stated care­
fully. If a married woman, her own given name should be stated; thus:
"Mrs. Anna May Smith," not "Mrs. John Smith."
This slip shall be made out and handled as follows:
Officer, Navy: Forward to Bureau of Naval Personnel.
Officer, Marine Corps: Forward to Commandant, Marine Corps.
Nurse: Forward to Bureau of Medicine and Surgery.
New beneficiary slips shall be executed and forwarded as above in all
cases of change in status of the grantor or in that of his or her bene•
ficiaries.
In any event, payment will be made to the widow or children, if any
whether designated or not, but not to a mother or other relative unles~
designated,

INSTRUCTIONS
This form must be sworn to before an officer of the
United States Navy authorized to administer oaths, or
before a notary public.
The full names and addresses of the beneficiaries must
be stated carefully. If a married woman, her own given
name should be stated: thus: "Mrs. Anna May Smith," not
"Mrs. John Smith."
This slip shall be made out and handled as follows:
Officer, Navy: Forward to Bureau of Naval Personnel.
Nurse: Forward to Bureau of Medicine and Surgery.
Enlisted men, Navy: In duplicate: one copy to Bureau
of Naval Personnel, one copy secured inside service rec­
ord. In cases of men entitled to money allowances for
quarters for enlisted men with dependents, t.1vo additional
copies, bearing the required certificate, to the Disbursing
Officer.
New beneficiary slips shall be executed and forwarded
as above in all cases of change in status of the granfor or
in that of his or her beneficiaries.
In any event, payment will be made to the widow or
children, if any, whether designated or not.
Normally casualty notification will be sent to all the
beneficiaries designat ed on this form. If, by reason of
(
unusual circumstances, other special arrangements ore
desired, a statement should be attached setting forth
full instructions including full names, addresses, and
relationships.

Subject: Leave of Absence — request for.
Le It is requested that I be granted thirty (30) days leave

in the United States to begin on or about April 15, 1943.

2. I have been on duty outside the United States since Nov-
aie 16, 1940 and have had no leave since March, 1940.

On October 25, 1939 I was transferred from the Naval Air
Station, Sitka, Alaska to Patrol Squadron Forty-five at the Naval Air

Station, San Diego, California. This squadron was redesignated VP Squad-

ron Fourteen and I was transferred from there on November 16, 1940 to
the Naval Air Station, Guantanamo Bay, Cuba where, on August 10, 1942,
I received orders to report. to the Rompnnden® Fifteenth Naval District
for assignment to duty.

h, If this request be granted, my address while in the
United States will be:
1210 S.E. 37th Avenue,
Portland, Oregon

From; Chief of Naval Personnel
To: CHPHOT, W-4 Delbert C. NEWHOUSE, USN
Pacific Fleet Photo-Triangulation Group

Via; Commander, Training Command, Pacific Fleet
Subj: Orders relieving you of all active duty incident to your retirement

Ref: (a) Sections 1 and 5, Chapter 14, BuPers Manual

1. Your request to be transferred to the retired list was approved by the Secretary
of the Navy, effective 1 April 1960.

2.‘ When directed by your commanding officer on 31 March 1960 you will regard
yourself detached from all duties which may have been assigned you and will proceed
to your home of selection. You will regard yourself relieved of all active duty in
the Navy effective at 2400 on date of detachment.

3. On 1 April 1960 you will be transferred to the retired list with the rank
of Chief Photographer, W-4 pursuant to the provisions of Title 10,
U.S. Code, Sections 1293, 1315 and IS Alka te

4, It having been determined that you served satisfactorily in the rank of
lieutenant you are hereby advanced, effective 1 April 1960

to the rank of lieutenant on the retired list and with the retired pay based on
the rank of Chief Photographer, W-!, pursuant to the provisions of Title 10, U. 8.
Code, Section 6151 as amended.

5. Attention is invited to references (a) and (b) concerning general inforastion
affecting the retirement of officers. ;

(ey Abie you are hospitalized at any time during the period preceding the date’ of your

retirement, you should advise the Chief of Navel Personnel by message eoncerning the
diagnosis, prognosis, and probable period of hospitalization.

7- You will not be required to take a new physical examination unless & material
change in your physical condition haa occurred subsequent to your recent retirement
physical examination. i

8. It is requested that you report your mail address in accordance with Articte
B-2206 (7), Bureau of Naval Personnel Manual. :

10. Enter in item llc, DD Form 214; BuPers ltr Pers-B72i-cbe-2 of 29 February 1960,
569, Voluntary retirement after twenty years' service - Warrant Officer - 10 USC 1293.

From: Chief of Naval Personnel
To: CHPHOT, W-4 Delbert C. NEWHOUSE, USN
Pacific Fleet Photo-Triangulation Group

Via: Commander, Training Command, Pacific Fleet.
Subj: Orders relieving you of all active duty incident to your retirement
Ref: (a) Sections 1 and 5, Chapter 14, BuPers Manual Ale

(b) BuPers Instruction 1811.14 (6)
1. Your request to be transferred to the retired list was approved by the Secretary
of the Navy, effective 1 April 1960.

2.‘ When directed by your commending officer on 31 March 1960 you will regard
yourself detached from all duties which may have been assigned you and will proceed
to your home of selection. You will regard yourself relieved of all active duty in
the Navy effective at 2400 on date of detachment.

3- On 1 April 1960 you will be transferred to the retired list with the rank
of Chief Photographer, w- pursuant to the provisions of Title 10, \

2. Your attention is invited to the requirements set forth in subparagraph 3k
of reference (a ) regarding a retirement physical examination. Your request
cannot be forwarded to the Secretary of the Navy for approval until a report
of such examination is received. If you have forwarded a report of pre­
retirement physical examination or a certified copy thereof to Pers-B52, pl.ease

3. This acknowledgment is not to be construed as being an indication of approval

Navy. Appropriate orders will be issued effecting your transfer to the Retired
List upon approval of your request by the Secretary of the Navy. It would be
advisable to exercise caution in making commitments in anticipation of voluntary
retirement until you are 1n receipt of retirement orders.
4. You are advised that, with certain exceptions, retired officers including
warrant officers of the Regular Navy are prohibited from holding a civilian
position or office with the Federal Government when either the retired pay o~
the rate of compensation fixed for the civilian position or office amounts to
$2500 or more per annum. Your specific attention is invited to the informa­
tion concerning the Dual Employment Statute, the Dual Compensation Statute,
and the conflict-of-interest restrictions of private employment activities,
~ontained in Chapters 1 and 2 of Part Eight of enclosure (1).

an annual physical examination within the four months preceeding receipt
of this letter, and no disqualifying defects were noted, he is to be
considered physically qualified for promotion and the appointment may be
delivered.
4. If subject officer has not had an annual physical examination within
the above stated time, or if he has had an examination wherein disqualifying
defects were noted, the corru.ianding officer shall direct him to report to a
medical officer for physical examination for promotion.
'lbe physical stand­
ards shall be the same as those for retention on active duty.
The findings
of the medical officer should read, "is (or is not) physically qualified for
continuation on active duty and for promotion to the grade of Chief Warrant
Officer W.4 "• Report of this physical examination will be. made to the Chief
of the Bureau of Medicine and Surgery on Standard Form 88.
~•

is not mentally, morally or professionally qualified or, if he is not ph?si­
cally qualified for promotion, enclosure (1) shall be returne~ to the Chief
of Naval Personnel, Attn: Pers B1311, with a report of the circumstances.

DEPARTMENT OF THE NAVY

WASHINGTON 25, D. C.
IN REPLY REFER TO
23 November 1949
From: Lieutenant Del bert c. Ne\7house, USN/177283/1350
'To:
Chief, Bureau of Naval Personnel
Via:
Chief, Bureau of Aeronautics
Subj: Information concerning termination of temporary appointment
Ref:
(b) BuPers C/L No. 148-49 dtd 15 Sept 1949
Encl: (1) Transcripts of portions of representative fitness reports
1. I received reference (a) on 25 October 1949 notifying me that my
temporary appointment as lieutenant would terminate on 31 December 19490
2. Reference (b) published the policy for the priority.of reversion
of temporary officers and asserted that it was highly desirable t hat
r educt ions be effected on a logical basis, considering both the best
interests of t he service and the most equitabl e consideration of the
temporary officers concerned. Paragraph 2(d) stated that temporary
officers, permanent commissioned warrant, would be reverted in ascending
order of r etirement dat es.
3.
Aft er r eceipt of reference (a), I have made a careful examination
of my correspondence and fitness report files in the Bureau of Naval
Personnel.
Mat er ial of a possibly adverse nature was disclosed by
this examination in that an officer 's fitness report dated 29 February
1948 was checked in the unfavorable box and was signed by Captain J.
C. Cronin, USN, then Commander Utility Wings, Pacific. A partial
transcript thereof is appended hereto as Exhibit "A" of Enclosure (1).
This was my first report from this command and covered the period from
23 October 1947 to 29 February 1948. I was transferred from the com­
mand on 1 July 1948 and a transcript of section 12 of my· next and final
fitness r eport from Captain Cronin is appended hereto as Exhibit 11Bn
of Enclosure (1).
4. The balance of all my fitness reports have ranged from above average
to superior and, almost without exception, have recommended retention
in temporary rank, transfer to regular commissioned rank, and promotion
when el igible. Exhibits "C" through "G" to Enclosure (1) are submitted

EXPLANATION OF SCORES
I.
The scores are given in percentiles. The percentile sc;res r un from 01 to 99 and
indicate the percent of candidates receiving a lowe-r score. Thus, if a candidate
has a percentile score 63, 63 percent of the candidates taking the test made a
lower score and 37 percent had the same or a higher score.
2. It should be _noted that the total score percentile is rrot a n average of the
percentiles for the sections, but indicates a candidate's relative standfog on the
basis of his total test score.
Candidates with a generaJ.ly high record on the test
will have a _percentile on the tobal score that is higher than the average of thek
percentiles on the sections.
Similarly, candid.ates with a generally low. record
on the. test will have a lower percentile on the total score than the average of
their section percentile scores.

Subject to the conditions stated below, the above individual was appointed by the President to the rank indicated •
above, for temporary service.
In the event all stated conditions be met, the appointment (unless expressly declined) is to be regarded for all purposes •
as having been accepted on the date made, without formal acceptance or oath of office, as provided in Section 5 of an
Act of Congress approved June 30, 1942, (Public No. 63.9 -
77th Congress).
•
CONDITIONS UNDER WHICH APPOINTMENT WOULD NOT BE EFFECTED:
1. Physical disqualification.
•
2. Withheld by commanding officer.
3. Declineo1
4.
(a) Under treatment in hospital;
•
(b) On sick leave;
(c)
Missing;
(d) Prisoner of war.

ence jacket unless notation appears hereon indicating appointment not effected.

INFORMATION AND INSTRUCTIONS CONCERNING THIS ELECTION FORM
(For Nonretired members)
1.
It is desired that all members with 17 years service creditable for basic pay ·purposes, active or inactive, either decline or
elect to provide certain annuities for their dependents under the provisions of the Uniformed Services Contingency Option Act of
1953 (Public Law 239-83rd. Congress). If you desire to receive a reduction in the retired pay which you may subsequently receive,
thereby providing an annuity for your dependents, you must elect the desired option (s) not later than 30 Apri l 1954 or prior
to completion of 18 years service creditable for basic pay purposes, whichever is later. The terms of the election may be modi­
fied or revoked prior to retirement, but any modification or revocation will not be effective if you retire within five years from
the date of such modification or revocation.
2.
If you do not desire to receive reduced retired pay to provide an annuity for your dependents, check the square opposite
"I do not desire to receive reduced retired pay in order to provide an annuity for my dependents" on the face of this form,
sign all copies of the form and return it to the Bureau of Naval Personnel, Washington 25, D. C. You may subsequently elect
to be covered by this Act, provided you submit such election to the Bureau of Naval Personnel, W ashington 25, D. C., ,!!2!
later than 30 April 1954 or prior to completion of 18 yc:irs service creditable for basic pay purposes. whichever is later.
3.
If you desire to receive reduced retired pay to provide an annuity for your dependents, fill out the form completely check­
ing desired option (s) ; a ll copies must be signed and notarized or attested.
4.
If either Option 2 or 3 is elected, it is required that all eligible children be named.­
5.
If should be noted that, under Option 3, all benefits are paid to the widow until her death or remarriage and thereafter
in equal shares to or on behalf of the surviving children remaining eligible at the time the payment is due. Consequently, if it
is desired that children of a former marriage receive payments concurrently with the widow, Options l and 2 should be elected.
6.
In indicating the place of dissolution of a former marriage, please include the city, county and state.
7.
The triplicate of this form is to be retained by you for your personal file. For active duty personnel, the original will be
forwarded to the Bureau of Naval Personnel, W ashington 25, D. C., and the duplicate is to be filed in your personnel jacket at
your station of duty. For members in the reserve components, the original and duplicate will be forwarded to the Bureau of
Naval Personnel, W ashington 25, D. C.

This is in further reply to your inquiry of 31 March 1959 concerning ay
Chief Warrant Officer Delbert C. Newhouse, United States Navy. es
Present plans are to detach Mr. Newhouse from the USS ESSEX (CVA-9) pire
in July of this year and to transfer him to the Fleet Camera Party, fer

Pacific at San Diego, California. Earlier detachment of Mr. Newhouse
is ae feasible due to the unavailability of a qualified replacement
officer.

foregoing information may be of service to you.

(Items for which no instructions are printed below are self-explanatory)

Ir Necessary, CONTINUE ANY ITEM IN "‘REMARKS” But
SPECIFY THE PARTICULAR ITEM BEING CONTINUED.

All items of this form Must be completed and MAINTAINED
Current by all officers and enlisted personnel in accordance
with the following: NAVY—BuPers Manual, Art. B2312;
MARINE CORPS~Par. 4019; PRAM; U. S. COAST 'GUARD—
Personnel Manual, Par. 13-B-14.

In the event you, the designator, should die, the persons or
agencies named in items 10, 11, 12, 13, 14, and 19 will be
notified unless item 15 shall indicate a person or petsons Not
to be notified.

Should you become critically or seriously ill, or incapacitated
to the extent that you cannot notify them, the persons named
in items 10, 11, 12, 13, and 14 will be notified unless item 15
shall indicate a person or persons Nor to be notified.

Payment of any benefits and disposition, of remains in case of
death shall be in accordance with applicable law.

Item 10.—Enter full name and address of wife or husband. For
wife, use given names, for example, “Marion Elizabeth Dawson.”
If the designator is single, divorced or widowed, so state.

Items 12 and 13.—The term ‘Father’ and/or ‘“‘Mother’’ shall
be interpreted to include “‘natural,’’ “step,” ‘adoptive’ and per-
son who stood in “loco parentis’’ to the designator for a period
of not less than 1 year at any time prior to designator’s entry
upon active service.

Item 14.—In the event that there is no other adult next of kin
other than those named in items 10, 12 and 14, you may name
a friend here. However, if a friend is named, he or she must be
so identified.

Item 14a. Enter under this item, the names of those persons
who are in fact actually receiving more than 50 percent of their
support from the designator. The information thus furnished
shall be used in connection with other determinations of depend-
ency'under existing or future laws.

Item 16.—Enter the name, address, and relationship of bene-
ficiary (or beneficiaries) whom you desire to receive the 6
months’ death gratuity in the event you are not survived by a
spouse or_eligible child(ren). The succession of eligible sur-
vivor's as set forth in the ‘‘Servicemen’s and Veterans’ Survivor

Benefits Act,” P. L. 881—84th Congress, is as follows: (1)
Spouse; (2) Children (without regard to their age or marital
status) in equal shares; (3) Parents or brothers or sisters (in-
cluding those of halfblood and those by adoption), when so
designated; (4) Parents in equal shares; or (5) Brothers and sis-

ters (including those of halfblood or those by adoption) in
equal shares.

NOTE:

The payment to either category (4) or (5) is in the event
that a designation is not made under category (3).

The term “parents’’ includes “natural parents,’’ ‘‘stepparents,”’
“adoptive parents’ and ‘persons who stood in “loco parentis” to
the designator for a period of not less than 1 year at any time
prior to the designator’s entry upon active service.

Item 17.—Enter name and address of the beneficiary or bene-
ficiaries whom you desire to receive any unpaid pay andvallow-
ances (Arrears of Pay) due you at the time of death. There are
no restrictions on who may be designated as a beneficiary or
beneficiaries to’ receive unpaid Pay! and allowances/afid! ofcé-a
designation-has beeh,made it is binding until superseded by the
completion of a later DD Form 93-1.

Item 18.—The:‘‘Missing Persons Act’’ provides that pay and
allowances continue to accrue to the pay account of any service
member for the period he is missing or captured and may be
paid to the dependents for support. This item reflects your de-
sires and is used as a guide in the disposition of your pay. Al-
lotments to dependents and insurance companies initiated prior
to entering a missing status are continued in effect unless
unusual circumstances indicate changes.

Item 21.—The person assigned, by the commanding officer, the
responsibility forsinterviewing the designator will, upon comple-
tion of the form, witness the designator’s signature and sign in
block 21. =

When directed by your Commanding Officer in Dece,mber 1957, you
will· regard yourself detached f roJ!!. duty with the Naval Air Technical Training
Unit, Naval Air Station, Pensacola, Florida, and from such other duty as may be
assigned you; will proceed to t he port in which the USS ESSEX (CVA-9) ma.y·be
and upon arrival ~eport to the CoJDl')Ja.!lding Officer_of that vessel for duty.
You are authorized to del ay until 10 January 1958, provided no excess leave is
involved, in reporting in compliance with these orders, such delay to count as
leave. Keep your old and new station advised of your address using Form NavPers
Since reimbursement for travel is based on travel required due to lo­
cation of vessel or fl~et command indicated above on the date of expiration of
delay, it is important to verify the location on that date.
Travel is chargeable to appropriation 1781453.18, MPN 1958, object class 0291
Expenditure Account 74120, Bureau Control 22/31600.

of these orders.

Ref:
(a) OPNAVINST 3590.4A, Subj: Awards for intratype battle efficiency
competition and insignia to denote excellence in certain
weapons _and operations
1. Commander Naval Air Force, U.S. Atlantic Fleet takes pleasure j_n
certifying the Operations Department of the USS ESSEX (CVS-9) as standing
first in the CVA class ship during fiscal year 1960.
2. The Commanding Officer is congratulated on the exemplary perform.auce
of the ESSEX's Operations Department .
3. The ESSEX is authorized to display the Operations Department Excellence
Insignia in accordance with reference (a).
4. Suitable entries will be made in the personnel records of the officers
and men of the ESSEX who materially contributed to the -earning of this
award.
The Commanding Officer is requested to forward a copy of this
letter to the command of those persom1el formerly serving on board during
fiscal year 1960, who significantly contributed to the winning of this
award.

From: Commanding Officer, USS ESSEX (CVA9)
Tos All Hands, USS ESSEX (CVA9)
Commander, air Task Group 201

Subj: Commendation for Operations performed during
Mediterranean and WesPac Deployment

1. The Commanding Officer is in receipt of many complimentary
communications-concerning the outstanding performance of ESSEX
and Air Task Group 201 during the period 2 February to

17 November 1958.

2e During this period ESSEX and her embarked Air Group served
as a striking unit of the Sixth Fleet in Mediterranean waters,
and her aircraft provided initial and sustained air support
for U. 5. Marine landings in Lebanon from 15 July to August
1958. These operations, under wartime conditions, were per-
formed by the men of ESSEX and ATG 201 with an outstanding
degree of professional skill and devotion to duty.

3. Following this, ESSEX was deployed on an emergency basis
to the Far East, where air operations in support of U. S.
National policy during the Formosa crisis were conducted in
the same outstanding manner.

4. Indicative of the performance of ESSEX and-her Air Group
are the following excerpts from various communications
received:

a. Ina letter of March 28, 1958, RADM N. S. HENDERSON,
O.B.E., RN, Vice Naval Deputy, Supreme Headquarters, Allied
Powers Europe who was on board observing operations, stated....
"But above all I took away an impression of a quietly effi-
cient ship with a very lively crew, all of whom seemed keen
and interested in the job. I was deeply impressed...."

INSTRUCTIONS

1. Enter each period of leave taken upon return or reporting for
duty.
2. On 1 July each year enter credit for leave accrued during past

fiscal year at rate of 2% days per month, not to exceed 30 days.
Reduce credit as necessary in order that new balance available
does not exceed 60 days. Unused credit cannot be carried for-
ward except as nofed below.

(First)

NAME (Last)

‘

3. In the case of leave commenced and extending beyond the end of
a fiscal year: Upon return or reporting for new duty, deduct leave
taken from balance available at commencement of leave period;
add leave credit accrued as of 30 June not to exceed 30 days.
New balance available may not exceed 60 days.

4. This record to be transferred with officer upon change of duty; com-
manding officer authenticate entries prior to detachment of officer.

weapons, and in dealing with the public in personal or official contacts, This space must not be left blank.

17. COMMENTS. In this section it is important that the outstanding characteristics of the officer, and any weaknesses which seriously affect his performance of duty, be reported. The
appraisal should be concise and concrete in terms of specific examples of performance from which the more general factors can be inferred. The appraisal will justify the marks assigned in

items 14, 15, and 16, above. In addition, the appraisal should include outstanding qualifications in any field such as the following: administration, planning, logistics, electronics, new
weapons, and in dealing with the public in personal or official contacts. This space must not be left blank.

CHPHOT NEWHOUSE has displayed consistent reliability. He accepts and carries out
his assignments and responsibilities in an excellent manner and with dispatch. He
has assumed responsibility for more jobs than would normally be assigned to him due
to a shortage of personnel, but his years of experience in the field of photography,

coupled with his amiable nature have enabled him to efficiently handle the additional

work, He is impeccable in his wearing of the uniform and in his military manner.

18. To WHat Decren HAS THE OFFICER REPORTED ON EXHIBITED THE FOLLOWING QUALITIES ?

(a) INTELLIGENCE
(With reference to the faculty of com-
prehension; mental acuteness.)

(b) JUDGMENT

(With reference to a discriminating
perception by which the values and
relations of things are mentally as-
serted.)

(c) INITIATIVE

(With reference to constructive think-
ing and resourcefulness; abilit;
intelligence to act on own res
bility.)

sults.)

(With reference to the faculty of di-
recting, controlling, and influencing
others in definite lines of action and
of maintaining discipline.)

(f) MORAL COURAGE

(With reference to that mental quality
which impels one to carry out the dic-
tates of his conscience and convictions
fearlessly.)

(g) COOPERATION

(With reference to the faculty of
working harmoniously with others to-
ward the accomplishment of common
duties.)

(h) LOYALTY

(¥Videlity, faithfulness, allegiance,
constancy—all with reference to a
cause and to higher authority.)

(With reference to maintenance of
purpose or undertaking in spite of
obstacles or discouragement.)

(With reference to the faculty of act-
ing instinctively in a logical manner
in difficult and unforeseen situations. )

(k) ENDURANCE

ing on under any and all conditions.)

12. Give in this space a clear, concise appraisal of the officer reported on and his performance of duty, including any worthy of special mention. Include recommendaticns as to promotion. Any statements of
“unsatisfactory performance, ability, character, or conduct must be referred to the officer for statement. Statements of a constructive nature which refer to minor imperfections or lack of qualifications do
not constitute an unsatisfactory report. For example: “This officer was a little slow in getting started but is now making good progress” or ‘This officer is well qualifted in his present duties but has had
no experience at sea’’ would not be unsatisfactory in nature.
